 succeeded in 341ms:
'use client'

// src/components/command-centre/wiki-graph/WikiGraphTile.tsx
//
// Command-deck tile for the Wiki Graph View (UNI-2304). Client-fetches the
// founder-scoped graph summary and links to the full interactive page.
// Error-contained: a failed fetch degrades to an honest message, never a fake
// count.

import { useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import Link from 'next/link'
import { SourceBadge, type SourceMode } from '@/components/command-centre/SourceBadge'

interface GraphSummary {
  pageCount: number
  edgeCount: number
  lastSync: string | null
}

export function WikiGraphTile() {
  const [summary, setSummary] = useState<GraphSummary | null>(null)
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true)
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null)

  useEffect(() => {
    let alive = true
    async function load() {
      try {
        const res = await fetch('/api/command-centre/wiki-graph')
        if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status}`)
        const data = (await res.json()) as GraphSummary
        if (alive) {
          setSummary({ pageCount: data.pageCount, edgeCount: data.edgeCount, lastSync: data.lastSync })
          setError(null)
        }
      } catch (e) {
        if (alive) setError(e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'load failed')
      } finally {
        if (alive) setLoading(false)
      }
    }
    load()
    return () => {
      alive = false
    }
  }, [])

  const mode: SourceMode = loading ? 'loading' : error || !summary ? 'degraded' : 'live'

  return (
    <section style={{ display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: 8 }}>
      <div style={{ display: 'flex', justifyContent: 'space-between', alignItems: 'center' }}>
        <h3 style={{ color: 'var(--deck-text)', fontSize: 14, fontWeight: 700, margin: 0 }}>Wiki Graph</h3>
        <SourceBadge mode={mode} label="Wiki" lastUpdatedAt={summary?.lastSync ?? undefined} />
      </div>

      {error ? (
        <p style={{ color: 'var(--deck-abort-text)', fontSize: 12, margin: 0 }}>Could not load wiki graph: {error}</p>
      ) : (
        <p style={{ color: 'var(--deck-muted)', fontSize: 12, margin: 0 }}>
          <b style={{ color: 'var(--deck-text)' }}>{summary?.pageCount ?? 0}</b> pages Â·{' '}
          <b style={{ color: 'var(--deck-text)' }}>{summary?.edgeCount ?? 0}</b> links in the knowledge base
        </p>
      )}

      <Link
        href="/command-centre/wiki-graph"
        style={{
          alignSelf: 'flex-start',
          fontSize: 12,
          padding: '5px 12px',
          borderRadius: 2,
          border: '1px solid var(--deck-line)',
          color: 'var(--deck-cyan-text)',
          textDecoration: 'none',
        }}
      >
        Open interactive graph â†’
      </Link>
    </section>
  )
}

**Primary Question**

The apparatus is still reactive in the important sense: it now has a clearer principle, but the implementation still overclaims what it can see. The coherent principle is: define the command-centre capability surface, require every reachable file and import to have provenance, fail closed when the baseline is unavailable, compare ported files against source by bounded static construct counts, and separately prove declared exemptions such as auth relocation. That is a real design, not just random test accumulation.

But it is not yet architecturally sound as a conformance apparatus because the boundary detectors are still regex and filesystem heuristics, and the current diff already contains a navigation form the route-existence check does not see. The test says “every internal href/fetch resolves,” but it only scans literal `href=`, literal `fetch(`, and `router.push/replace` prefixes in [command-centre-readonly.test.ts](D:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/__tests__/command-centre-readonly.test.ts:235). The new index page uses `<Link href={d.href}>` in [page.tsx](D:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/app/(main)/command-centre/page.tsx:50), with path literals stored in `DECKS`. Those current paths are valid, so this is not a present 404. It is still the same class of defect as prior rounds: coverage reads wider than it is. A future bad `DECKS` href would pass this route-existence check.

That makes the route check a reactive patch in miniature. Scanning navigation “forms” by regex is not a design that can be completed; Next navigation can appear as `<Link href={expr}>`, object hrefs, local arrays, helper components, `window.location`, callbacks passed down as props, server redirects, forms/actions, or wrappers around `router`. The sound version is either AST-based extraction with limited dataflow for local constants and wrapper components, or runtime route exercising from rendered output. Regex can be useful as a cheap tripwire, but it should not be described as proving every internal navigation resolves.

The import provenance half is stronger. Seeding from both command-centre pages and `app/api/command-centre` route files closes the specific API-route blindness, and the bidirectional map checks plus missing-target-file assertion close the phantom-entry class for static imports discovered by the graph. The remaining structural misses are known: dynamic imports, computed requires, package side effects, CSS fetches, environment-driven behavior, route handlers outside the seeded subtree, and semantics where the count stays unchanged but the target changes.

The positive controls and fail-closed checks are load-bearing, not decorative. Baseline reachability, populated import sets, auth controls, and exact delta transitions prevent vacuous green runs. They do not surface the next blind spot by themselves; they only prove the current detectors are alive. The next blind spot still comes from a reviewer unless the harness moves from regex form-scanning to a declared coverage model with parser/runtime checks.

**Axis 1 - Standards**

No hard TypeScript/React/Next.js standards violation blocks the feature code itself. Removing the wiki node click is the right standards call: [WikiGraphCanvas.tsx](D:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/components/command-centre/wiki-graph/WikiGraphCanvas.tsx:308) removes a navigation that previously targeted `/founder/wiki/${slug}`, and the page caption no longer advertises “click to open.” That avoids a misleading interactive affordance.

The inherited anonymous `pointerleave` leak remains at [WikiGraphCanvas.tsx](D:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/components/command-centre/wiki-graph/WikiGraphCanvas.tsx:337). Shipping a known listener leak is not ideal React practice, but the file records it as inherited and deliberately unfixed under the “faithful port” rule. The lint suppression for `react-hooks/refs` is scoped to one line at [WikiGraphCanvas.tsx](D:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/components/command-centre/wiki-graph/WikiGraphCanvas.tsx:385), so a new refs violation elsewhere should still fail lint.

The proxy split is correct App Router boundary behavior: anonymous pages redirect, anonymous APIs 401. The route uses `NextResponse.json` and does not expose the catch-all error message.

**Axis 2 - Spec**

Attempt 3’s API-root fix is sound for this instance: current command-centre App Router surface has four pages and one API route, and `importGraph()` now seeds both `app/(main)/command-centre/**/page.tsx` and `app/api/command-centre/**/route.ts`. It is not generally complete for future capability surfaces like nested `layout.tsx`, `not-found.tsx`, colocated server actions, or middleware/proxy behavior; those need explicit roots or separate tests.

The response to attempt 2 mostly closes the stale import-map class for static imports: no phantom entries, no unmapped actual imports, and no `file:` target that is absent. `no-source-baseline` is legitimate for rebuilt/target-native files, but its constraint is still declared in the same JSON file, so it is controlled circularity rather than independent proof.

Blocking spec finding: the route-existence apparatus still overclaims. The test scans literal forms, while the diff includes `<Link href={d.href}>`. The current `DECKS` values are valid, but the test would not catch a bad one. That is another same-class “coverage wider than reality” failure.

Verification: `npx tsc --noEmit` passed. Both Vitest commands failed to start with `spawn EPERM` loading Vite config, matching the brief’s caveat. `npm run build` also failed locally with EPERM unlinking `.next/app-path-routes-manifest.json`, so I did not independently confirm the claimed green loop.

VERDICT: FAIL — route-existence coverage still overclaims; it misses the diff’s `<Link href={d.href}>` navigation form.
